**Create `ui/src/components/BrowserCall.tsx`:**

```tsx
import { useQuery } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { Globe } from "lucide-react";

import { commands } from "@/api/commands";
import { browserLine, screenshotName } from "@/lib/browser";

/// A call of the built-in browser in a transcript (browser spec section 7): one line that says
/// what it did, its result folded under it, and a screenshot it took shown as an image.
export function BrowserCall(props: {
  runId: string;
  tool: string;
  input: unknown;
  result: { output: string; isError: boolean } | null;
  /// The run is still going: a call without a result is running.
  active: boolean;
}) {
  const { result } = props;
  const shot = result && !result.isError ? screenshotName(result.output) : null;
  const image = useQuery({
    queryKey: ["browser-screenshot", props.runId, shot],
    queryFn: () => commands.browserScreenshot(props.runId, shot ?? ""),
    enabled: shot !== null,
    staleTime: Infinity,
  });
  const outcome =
    result === null ? (props.active ? "running" : "no result") : result.isError ? "failed" : "done";
  return (
    <div className="flex flex-col gap-1">
      <details className="rounded-md border px-2 py-1">
        <summary className="flex cursor-pointer items-center gap-2 text-xs">
          <Globe aria-hidden className="size-3.5 shrink-0" />
          <span className="min-w-0 flex-1 truncate">
            {browserLine(props.tool, props.input, result)}
          </span>
          <span className={result?.isError ? "text-destructive" : "text-muted-foreground"}>
            {outcome}
          </span>
        </summary>
        {result && (
          <pre
            className={`mt-1 max-h-80 overflow-auto text-xs whitespace-pre-wrap ${
              result.isError ? "text-destructive" : "text-muted-foreground"
            }`}
          >
            {result.output}
          </pre>
        )}
      </details>
      {image.data && (
        <img
          src={image.data}
          alt="Screenshot of the dot's browser"
          className="max-h-80 self-start rounded-md border"
        />
      )}
    </div>
  );
}
```

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: the Deviations table of this plan; `git log --format=%B master..feat/browser` for fixes recorded by Tasks 1 to 11.
- Produces: specs that match the code.

- [ ] **Step 1: Edit the specs**

**Append to `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-21-browser-design.md`:**

```markdown

## 13. Changes made while building

The plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-21-browser.md`) changed these from the first version of this spec, after measuring Edge 155 and `@playwright/mcp@0.0.83` on this PC:

- Edge also starts with `--disable-sync`, `--disable-backgrounding-occluded-windows`, `--disable-renderer-backgrounding`, `--disable-background-timer-throttling` and `--disable-features=CalculateNativeWinOcclusion`, and opens `about:blank`. A fresh profile signed in to the person's Microsoft account and synced their extensions into the dot's profile; behind other windows, every click timed out (section 2.1).
- Edge's `msedge.exe` hands over to the browser and exits at once, so Dots follows a dot's Edge by its port, kept in `<data_dir>/browser/<dot_id>/port` (a Dots that starts again finds an open Edge), and closes it with `PUT /json/close/<id>` for each page. A profile is in use when its `lockfile` cannot be opened for writing (section 2.1).
- The Playwright server also gets `--snapshot-mode none`, so acts do not write snapshot files into `downloads/`; the model takes snapshots with `browser_snapshot`, which still answers inline. Its tool list is the captured one (`browser/tools.json`), served without starting the server; its `list_changed` is not followed (section 2.2).
- Acts name their element with `target`, a snapshot reference or a selector; `ref` does not exist in 0.0.83. A password field shows as `textbox "Password"`, so the name decides (section 3.5).
- The page an act lands on is the one Playwright reported as current last (its `### Page` section or `(current)` tab), counted only while Edge's `/json/list` has exactly that address; otherwise the act is on no site and asks. `/json/list` is ordered by what was last in front, not by Playwright's tab. Only Playwright's own result sections move the current page: console messages, found text and the results of code cannot (section 3.2).
- `browser_drop` with `paths` is dangerous, as an upload; `browser_handle_dialog` with `accept: false` is a read (section 3.1).
- "Always allow risky actions on <site>" saves `BrowserRisky(<site>)` alone, and an allow `BrowserRisky(<site>)` also approves the site. For browser calls a deny rule wins, then an ask rule (a `Browser(<site>)` ask keeps that site asking under `Browser(*)`), then an allow rule. A deny rule on the tool's own name (`mcp__browser__*`) refuses it too (sections 3.6 and 6).
- A live approval whose page changed while it waited is not performed either: "The page changed while your browser action waited for approval, so it was not performed. Take a snapshot and do it again if it is still right." (section 3.6).
- The card's data (`action`, `site`, `page`, `labels`, `risky`, `dangerous`) is stored in the approval's input under `dots_browser`; a key of that name in the model's input is dropped first. A card offers no rule for a page with no site or a dangerous call (section 3.6).
- Edge saves downloads to `<data_dir>/browser/<dot_id>/downloads` (written into the profile's `Default/Preferences`); Dots moves each finished file into the run's `downloads/` after each browser call and when the run ends. Over CDP, Playwright's output folder does not catch downloads (section 4).
- A performed call's result starts with `Dots: <line>` (`Opened shop.com/cart`, `Clicked "Add to cart"`, `Typed into "Search"`, `Took a screenshot`, `Downloaded statement.pdf`); the run view shows those lines. A screenshot is read back with the command `browser_screenshot(run_id, name)`, only under the name Playwright gives one (sections 7 and 8).
- A new `Settings.edge_path` is confirmed in a native dialog ("Change the Edge program?"), as a new Claude program is (section 6).
- A package that cannot be fetched fails with "The browser could not start: <MCP error>"; npm's own text goes to the app log (section 5).
- A Library MCP server the dot uses under the name `browser` keeps it; the built-in browser is then left out of the run, with a notice (section 2.2).
- The fake Playwright server of the tests is `fake-mcp` with a script; `fake-browser` stands in for Edge (section 9).
```

**In `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-dots-design.md`, replace:**

```markdown
    `--allowedTools` / `--disallowedTools`, because the CLI has no such tools, and Dots' own
    `dots` server checks each call against them on the Claude engine.
```

**with:**

```markdown
    `--allowedTools` / `--disallowedTools`, because the CLI has no such tools, and Dots' own
    `dots` server checks each call against them on the Claude engine.
  - From the browser spec, the rule kinds `Browser(<site>)` and `BrowserRisky(<site>)` (`allow`,
    `ask` or `deny`; `<site>` a lower-case host or `*`). Dots' browser gate reads them for the
    `browser` tools before the dot's other rules (deny, then ask, then allow); no preset carries
    them and the CLI never sees them (`2026-10-21-browser-design.md` sections 3 and 6).
```

**In `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-15-mcp-skills-plugins-design.md`, replace:**

```markdown
Names refer to Library items. A plugin's MCP servers come with the plugin; they are not ticked separately. `validate()` refuses a name that is not in the Library. Deleting a Library item that dots use asks first and names those dots; on delete, the name is removed from their `uses`.
```

**with:**

```markdown
Names refer to Library items. A plugin's MCP servers come with the plugin; they are not ticked separately. `validate()` refuses a name that is not in the Library. Deleting a Library item that dots use asks first and names those dots; on delete, the name is removed from their `uses`.

### The built-in browser

From the browser spec (`2026-10-21-browser-design.md` section 2.2), a run of a dot with `browser` on has one more MCP server, started by Dots at the run's first browser call: the built-in `browser` (`@playwright/mcp@0.0.83`). Its tools are `mcp__browser__<tool>`, reached through the gateway route `/mcp/s/browser` on the Claude engine and bridged on the local engine. It is not a Library item: its tool list is the pinned one, not fingerprinted, and Dots' browser gate decides each call instead of section 4. A Library server the dot uses under the name `browser` keeps the name, and the built-in browser is left out of that run.
```

```markdown
The tool descriptions (shared by both engines in `checkins.rs`, as `context::DESCRIPTION` is) say when to use each: `notify` for progress the person would want to know now, sparingly; `ask` only when the dot cannot go on well without the person's judgement; `follow_up` for work that has to wait (a build, a reply, a date).
```

**with:**

```markdown
The tool descriptions (shared by both engines in `checkins.rs`, as `context::DESCRIPTION` is) say when to use each: `notify` for progress the person would want to know now, sparingly; `ask` only when the dot cannot go on well without the person's judgement; `follow_up` for work that has to wait (a build, a reply, a date).

From the browser spec (`2026-10-21-browser-design.md` section 4), a dot that browses hands over to the person with `ask` when a page needs a login, a code or a CAPTCHA ("Please <do it> in the browser window, then answer done."): the person does it in the dot's visible Edge window and answers, the answer resumes the run, and the login stays in the dot's profile.
```

### Task 13: The smoke test of browser use

Spec section 9 asks for a smoke with real Edge and real Playwright MCP, a local dot and a Claude dot: reading a public page, a first-time site approval, adding to cart on a demo shop with "Place order" asking, a login handover through `ask`, and a download landing in the workspace. As in `docs/verification/2026-10-21-checkins-followups-smoke.md`, the agent drives a debug build with a local model (Part A); Part B lists what a person runs at the machine, and the agent may run it too when the person asks. The record goes to `docs/verification/2026-10-21-browser-smoke.md`, in the form of the previous smoke: a header (date, build and commit, model, Edge and Playwright versions, how many steps passed, where the screenshots are), "What this covers", "How the agent steps were run", then each step with its commands, "Expect:" and "Result:" from what happened, and the findings.

**Files:**

- Create: `docs/verification/2026-10-21-browser-smoke.md`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: a debug build (`export PATH="/c/Program Files/LLVM/bin:$PATH"; bun run tauri build --debug --no-bundle` in the repository root), a fresh `DOTS_DATA_DIR` and `WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER` in the run's scratchpad, `tools/smoke/webview.mjs`, Ollama with `qwen2.5:7b` at `http://127.0.0.1:11434`, Microsoft Edge, Node.js.
- Produces: the smoke record.

How the agent steps are run: as in the previous smoke (the app started with the fresh folders, driven through WebView2's debugging port with `tools/smoke/webview.mjs`, helpers `new_dot`, `run_now`, `settled`, `calls`, `said`), with `npx -y @playwright/mcp@0.0.83 --version` run once first so the package is fetched before a run waits on it. Every dot's profile is under the fresh data folder (`<DOTS_DATA_DIR>/browser/<dot_id>/profile`); the person's own Edge profile is never used, and every Edge the smoke opened is closed at its end (`tasklist` shows no `msedge.exe` whose command line names the data folder). The demo shop is a local page served for the smoke from the scratchpad, so its labels are known:

```bash
mkdir -p "$S/shop" && cat > "$S/shop/serve.mjs" <<'SHOP'
import http from 'node:http';
const page = (title, body) => `<!doctype html><title>${title}</title>${body}`;
http.createServer((req, res) => {
  if (req.url.startsWith('/statement.pdf')) {
    res.writeHead(200, {'content-type': 'application/pdf', 'content-disposition': 'attachment; filename=statement.pdf'});
    return res.end('%PDF-1.4 demo');
  }
  const pages = {
    '/': page('Demo shop', '<h1>Demo shop</h1><button onclick="document.title=\'added\'">Add to cart</button> <a href="/cart">Cart</a> <a href="/login">Log in</a> <a href="/statement.pdf">Download statement</a>'),
    '/cart': page('Cart', '<h1>Cart</h1><p>1 item</p><button onclick="document.title=\'ordered\'">Place order</button>'),
    '/login': page('Log in', '<form onsubmit="document.cookie=\'who=me; max-age=86400\'; location=\'/\'; return false"><label>Email <input name="email"></label> <label>Password <input type="password" name="pw"></label> <button>Log in</button></form>'),
  };
  res.writeHead(200, {'content-type': 'text/html'});
  res.end(pages[req.url] ?? pages['/']);
}).listen(47999, '127.0.0.1');
SHOP
node "$S/shop/serve.mjs" &
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Agent: the status, and Edge where Settings says.** Open Settings. Expect: the Browser section shows "Microsoft Edge found" with `C:\Program Files (x86)\Microsoft\Edge\Application\msedge.exe` and "Node.js found" with the `npx.cmd` path. Type a wrong Edge path and save: the system dialog "Change the Edge program?" asks (with `DOTS_CONFIRM=yes` it is accepted); a browser call of any dot then fails with "Microsoft Edge was not found; browser use needs it (Settings → Edge path)." and the run goes on. Clear the path again.

- [ ] **Step 2: Agent: a local dot reads a public page, after a first-time site approval.** A local dot `reader` (folder workspace, `approval_wait_secs: 120`, Browser on, no site) with the instructions "Open https://example.com with the browser, take a snapshot, and tell me the page's main heading." Run it now. Expect: a visible Edge window opens on the dot's own profile (`--user-data-dir=<DOTS_DATA_DIR>\browser\<dot_id>\profile`, its command line read with `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`); the Inbox card reads "reader wants to open **example.com**" with "Allow once", "Always allow on example.com" and "Deny"; click "Always allow on example.com". The run view shows "Opened example.com" and "Read the page", the summary names "Example Domain", the editor's Sites list shows `example.com`, and the Edge window closes when the run ends.

- [ ] **Step 3: Agent: add to cart on the demo shop, and "Place order" asks.** Instructions of `reader`: "Open http://127.0.0.1:47999/, take a snapshot, click Add to cart, open the cart, take a snapshot and click Place order." Expect: the card "reader wants to open **127.0.0.1**"; approve it with "Always allow on 127.0.0.1" (the Sites list gains `127.0.0.1`). Then "Clicked "Add to cart"" without a card, and the card "reader wants to click **"Place order"** on **127.0.0.1**" with "Allow once", "Always allow on 127.0.0.1", "Always allow risky actions on 127.0.0.1" and "Deny". Deny: the run is told "Denied by the reviewer." and the page title stays "Cart". Run it again and choose Allow once: the run view shows "Clicked "Place order"" and the page title becomes "ordered".

- [ ] **Step 4: Agent: a download lands in the workspace.** Instructions: "Open http://127.0.0.1:47999/, take a snapshot and click Download statement, then take a snapshot." Expect: the workspace's `downloads/statement.pdf` exists and holds `%PDF-1.4 demo`; the run view shows "Downloaded statement.pdf"; nothing new is in the person's own Downloads folder.

- [ ] **Step 5: Agent: the login handover asks, and the password is never typed.** Instructions: "Open http://127.0.0.1:47999/login, take a snapshot, type me@example.com into Email and log in." Expect: typing into "Email" goes through; a `browser_type` into "Password", if the model tries it, answers "Dots does not type passwords or codes. Ask the person to enter it in the browser window, with your ask tool."; the dot calls `ask` ("Please … in the browser window, then answer done."), and the question is in the Inbox. Answering it (done) is Part B, by a person at the window.

- [ ] **Step 6: Agent: a window closed during a run, and a profile in use.** (a) During Step 3's run, close the dot's Edge window with the mouse (or `Stop-Process` on its process): the next browser call starts Edge again on the same profile and the dot sees a fresh page. (b) Start `msedge.exe --user-data-dir=<that profile>` by hand, then run the dot: the call fails with "This dot's browser profile is in use by another Edge window; close it and try again."; close that Edge.

- [ ] **Step 7: Person (or the agent on request): a real Claude dot and the handover.** With a real `claude` CLI, a Claude dot with Browser on and the instructions of Steps 3 and 5. Expect: `--allowedTools` holds `mcp__browser__*` and `--mcp-config` an entry `browser` on `/mcp/s/browser`; "Place order" asks and passes after "Always allow risky actions on 127.0.0.1" (the policy gains `BrowserRisky(127.0.0.1)`); for the login the dot asks, the person types the password in the Edge window, answers "done", the run resumes, and a later run of the same dot is still logged in (the shop's `who=me` cookie in the dot's profile). An approval decided after the run parked is not performed: the resumed run says "Your browser action was approved after the page may have changed. Take a snapshot and do it again if it is still right."

Write `docs/verification/2026-10-21-browser-smoke.md` from what happened in Steps 1 to 6 (each step's commands, "Expect:" and "Result:", with run ids and the texts seen), Step 7 as run or as open for the person, and the findings (a Dots defect is fixed in its own commit before this one, and named here). Stop the demo shop's server and every Edge the smoke opened.
